🔹 Block 9: The Highest Ideals

Original Text:

The life and sacrifice of Major Somnath Sharma embody the highest ideals of duty, honour, and patriotism. He has been a source of inspiration for soldiers and citizens alike. Most importantly, his story is a reminder of the sacrifices made by countless men and women in defence of the motherland and highlights the profound sense of duty that inspired them.

English-Odia Pronunciation (ଇଂରାଜୀ ଉଚ୍ଚାରଣ):

ଦ ଲାଇଫ୍ ଆଣ୍ଡ ସାକ୍ରିଫାଇସ୍ ଅଫ୍ ମେଜର୍ ସୋମନାଥ ଶର୍ମା ଏମ୍ବଡି ଦ ହାଇଏଷ୍ଟ୍ ଆଇଡିଆଲ୍ସ ଅଫ୍ ଡ୍ୟୁଟି, ଅନର୍, ଆଣ୍ଡ ପ୍ୟାଟ୍ରିଅଟିଜମ୍। ହି ହ୍ୟାଜ୍ ବିନ୍ ଏ ସୋର୍ସ ଅଫ୍ ଇନସ୍ପିରେସନ୍ ଫର୍ ସୋଲଜର୍ସ ଆଣ୍ଡ ସିଟିଜେନ୍ସ ଏଲାଇକ୍। ମୋଷ୍ଟ ଇମ୍ପୋର୍ଟାଣ୍ଟଲି, ହିଜ୍ ଷ୍ଟୋରୀ ଇଜ୍ ଏ ରିମାଇଣ୍ଡର୍ ଅଫ୍ ଦ ସାକ୍ରିଫାଇସେସ୍ ମେଡ୍ ବାଇ କାଉଣ୍ଟଲେସ୍ ମେନ୍ ଆଣ୍ଡ ୱିମେନ୍ ଇନ୍ ଡିଫେନ୍ସ ଅଫ୍ ଦ ମଦରଲ୍ୟାଣ୍ଡ ଆଣ୍ଡ ହାଇଲାଇଟ୍ସ ଦ ପ୍ରୋଫାଉଣ୍ଡ ସେନ୍ସ ଅଫ୍ ଡ୍ୟୁଟି ଦ୍ୟାଟ୍ ଇନସ୍ପାୟାର୍ଡ ଦେମ୍।

Odia Meaning (ଓଡ଼ିଆ ଅର୍ଥ):

ମେଜର ସୋମନାଥ ଶର୍ମାଙ୍କ ଜୀବନ ଏବଂ ବଳିଦାନ କର୍ତ୍ତବ୍ୟ, ସମ୍ମାନ ଏବଂ ଦେଶପ୍ରେମର ସର୍ବୋଚ୍ଚ ଆଦର୍ଶକୁ ପ୍ରତିପାଦିତ କରେ (ପ୍ରମାଣିତ କରେ)। ସେ ସୈନିକ ଏବଂ ନାଗରିକ ସମସ୍ତଙ୍କ ପାଇଁ ପ୍ରେରଣାର ଏକ ଉତ୍ସ ହୋଇ ରହିଆସିଛନ୍ତି। ସବୁଠାରୁ ଗୁରୁତ୍ୱପୂର୍ଣ୍ଣ କଥା ହେଉଛି, ତାଙ୍କର କାହାଣୀ ମାତୃଭୂମିର ସୁରକ୍ଷା ପାଇଁ ଅଗଣିତ ପୁରୁଷ ଓ ମହିଳାମାନେ ଦେଇଥିବା ବଳିଦାନକୁ ମନେ ପକାଇଦିଏ ଏବଂ ସେମାନଙ୍କୁ ଅନୁପ୍ରାଣିତ କରିଥିବା କର୍ତ୍ତବ୍ୟର ଗଭୀର ଭାବନାକୁ ଉଜାଗର କରେ।

 ଏହି ବିଷୟରେ (MCQ Test) https:///mcq ରେ ନିଜର ଦକ୍ଷତା ପରୀକ୍ଷା କରନ୍ତୁ

English Meaning:

Major Somnath Sharma's life perfectly represents what it means to be dutiful, honorable, and patriotic. His actions inspire both the army and normal citizens. Above all, his story reminds us of the countless brave people who have sacrificed their lives to protect our country, driven by a deep and powerful sense of duty.

 

Word Meanings (ଶବ୍ଦାର୍ଥ) [Easy to Hard]:

  • Embody (ଏମ୍ବଡି): ପ୍ରତିପାଦିତ କରିବା / ମୂର୍ତ୍ତିମନ୍ତ କରିବା (Be an expression of or give a tangible or visible form to an idea)

  • Ideals (ଆଇଡିଆଲ୍ସ): ଆଦର୍ଶ (Principles or standards of perfection)

  • Patriotism (ପ୍ୟାଟ୍ରିଅଟିଜମ୍): ଦେଶପ୍ରେମ (Devotion to one's country)

  • Countless (କାଉଣ୍ଟଲେସ୍): ଅଗଣିତ (Too many to be counted)

  • Defence (ଡିଫେନ୍ସ): ସୁରକ୍ଷା / ପ୍ରତିରକ୍ଷା (Protection against attack)

  • Profound (ପ୍ରୋଫାଉଣ୍ଡ): ଗଭୀର (Very great or intense)

🔹 Block 10: The Enduring Legacy

Original Text:

In the quiet of the Kashmir Valley, where the echoes of D Company's battle with infiltrators have long since faded, the story of Major Somnath Sharma remains a beacon of courage and sacrifice. His valour ensured that the tricolour would continue to fly over Srinagar, and his name is forever etched in the golden pages of India's military history. The life and legacy of Major Somnath Sharma stands as a poignant reminder of the price of freedom and the enduring spirit of those who fought to protect it. His story, marked by selflessness and bravery, will continue to inspire and guide generations to come, ensuring that his sacrifice is never to be forgotten. His name is etched on the Honour Wall of the National War Memorial.

English-Odia Pronunciation (ଇଂରାଜୀ ଉଚ୍ଚାରଣ):

ଇନ୍ ଦ କ୍ୱାଏଟ୍ ଅଫ୍ ଦ କାଶ୍ମୀର ଭ୍ୟାଲି, ହ୍ୱେର୍ ଦ ଇକୋଜ୍ ଅଫ୍ ଡି କମ୍ପାନିଜ୍ ବ୍ୟାଟଲ୍ ୱିଥ୍ ଇନଫିଲଟ୍ରେଟର୍ସ ହ୍ୟାଭ୍ ଲଙ୍ଗ୍ ସିନ୍ସ ଫେଡେଡ୍, ଦ ଷ୍ଟୋରୀ ଅଫ୍ ମେଜର୍ ସୋମନାଥ ଶର୍ମା ରିମେନ୍ସ ଏ ବିକନ୍ ଅଫ୍ କରେଜ୍ ଆଣ୍ଡ ସାକ୍ରିଫାଇସ୍। ହିଜ୍ ଭାଲୋର୍ ଏନସିଓର୍ଡ ଦ୍ୟାଟ୍ ଦ ଟ୍ରାଇକଲର୍ ଉଡ୍ କଣ୍ଟିନ୍ୟୁ ଟୁ ଫ୍ଲାଇ ଓଭର୍ ଶ୍ରୀନଗର୍, ଆଣ୍ଡ ହିଜ୍ ନେମ୍ ଇଜ୍ ଫରେଭର୍ ଏଚଡ୍ ଇନ୍ ଦ ଗୋଲ୍ଡେନ୍ ପେଜେସ୍ ଅଫ୍ ଇଣ୍ଡିଆଜ୍ ମିଲିଟାରୀ ହିଷ୍ଟ୍ରି। ଦ ଲାଇଫ୍ ଆଣ୍ଡ ଲେଗାସି ଅଫ୍ ମେଜର୍ ସୋମନାଥ ଶର୍ମା ଷ୍ଟାଣ୍ଡସ୍ ଆଜ୍ ଏ ପଏଗନାଣ୍ଟ ରିମାଇଣ୍ଡର୍ ଅଫ୍ ଦ ପ୍ରାଇସ୍ ଅଫ୍ ଫ୍ରିଡମ୍ ଆଣ୍ଡ ଦ ଏଣ୍ଡ୍ୟୁରିଂ ସ୍ପିରିଟ୍ ଅଫ୍ ଦୋଜ୍ ହୁ ଫଟ୍ ଟୁ ପ୍ରୋଟେକ୍ଟ ଇଟ୍। ହିଜ୍ ଷ୍ଟୋରୀ, ମାର୍କଡ୍ ବାଇ ସେଲ୍ଫଲେସନେସ୍ ଆଣ୍ଡ ବ୍ରେଭେରୀ, ୱିଲ୍ କଣ୍ଟିନ୍ୟୁ ଟୁ ଇନସ୍ପାୟାର୍ ଆଣ୍ଡ ଗାଇଡ୍ ଜେନେରେସନ୍ସ ଟୁ କମ୍, ଏନସିଓରିଂ ଦ୍ୟାଟ୍ ହିଜ୍ ସାକ୍ରିଫାଇସ୍ ଇଜ୍ ନେଭର୍ ଟୁ ବି ଫରଗଟେନ୍। ହିଜ୍ ନେମ୍ ଇଜ୍ ଏଚଡ୍ ଅନ୍ ଦ ଅନର୍ ୱାଲ୍ ଅଫ୍ ଦ ନ୍ୟାସନାଲ୍ ୱାର୍ ମେମୋରିଆଲ୍।

 

Odia Meaning (ଓଡ଼ିଆ ଅର୍ଥ):

କାଶ୍ମୀର ଉପତ୍ୟକାର ଶାନ୍ତ ପରିବେଶରେ, ଯେଉଁଠାରେ ଅନୁପ୍ରବେଶକାରୀଙ୍କ ସହ 'ଡି କମ୍ପାନୀ'ର ଯୁଦ୍ଧର ପ୍ରତିଧ୍ୱନି ବହୁ ପୂର୍ବରୁ ଲିଭିଯାଇଛି (ଫିକା ପଡିଯାଇଛି), ସେଠାରେ ମେଜର ସୋମନାଥ ଶର୍ମାଙ୍କ କାହାଣୀ ସାହସ ଏବଂ ବଳିଦାନର ଏକ ଆଲୋକବର୍ତ୍ତିକା ହୋଇ ରହିଛି। ତାଙ୍କର ବୀରତ୍ୱ ନିଶ୍ଚିତ କରିଥିଲା ଯେ ଶ୍ରୀନଗର ଉପରେ ତ୍ରିରଙ୍ଗା ପତାକା ଉଡ଼ିବା ଜାରି ରହିବ, ଏବଂ ତାଙ୍କ ନାମ ଭାରତୀୟ ସାମରିକ ଇତିହାସର ସୁବର୍ଣ୍ଣ ପୃଷ୍ଠାରେ ସବୁଦିନ ପାଇଁ ଲିପିବଦ୍ଧ ହୋଇରହିବ। ମେଜର ସୋମନାଥ ଶର୍ମାଙ୍କ ଜୀବନ ଏବଂ ତାଙ୍କର ଅବଦାନ ସ୍ୱାଧୀନତାର ମୂଲ୍ୟ ଏବଂ ଏହାକୁ ରକ୍ଷା କରିବାକୁ ଲଢ଼ିଥିବା ବ୍ୟକ୍ତିଙ୍କ ଅତୁଟ ମନୋବଳର ଏକ ହୃଦୟସ୍ପର୍ଶୀ ସ୍ମାରକୀ ଭାବରେ ଠିଆ ହୋଇଛି। ନିଃସ୍ୱାର୍ଥପରତା ଏବଂ ବୀରତ୍ୱରେ ପରିପୂର୍ଣ୍ଣ ତାଙ୍କର କାହାଣୀ ଆଗାମୀ ପିଢ଼ିକୁ ପ୍ରେରଣା ଓ ମାର୍ଗଦର୍ଶନ ଦେଉଥିବ, ଏବଂ ନିଶ୍ଚିତ କରିବ ଯେ ତାଙ୍କର ବଳିଦାନ କେବେବି ଭୁଲାଯାଇ ପାରିବ ନାହିଁ। ଜାତୀୟ ସମର ସ୍ମାରକୀ (National War Memorial) ର ସମ୍ମାନ ପ୍ରାଚୀରରେ (Honour Wall) ତାଙ୍କ ନାମ ଖୋଦେଇ କରାଯାଇଛି।

 

English Meaning:

Even though the battle is long over and the Kashmir Valley is quiet again, Major Somnath Sharma's heroic story still shines brightly. Because of his bravery, the Indian flag continues to fly over Srinagar today. His name is permanently written in India's golden history. His selfless life is a sad but beautiful reminder of how much freedom costs and the strong spirit of the soldiers who protect it. His unforgettable sacrifice will keep inspiring future generations forever. Today, you can see his name proudly carved on the Honour Wall at the National War Memorial in New Delhi.

Word Meanings (ଶବ୍ଦାର୍ଥ) [Easy to Hard]:

  • Faded (ଫେଡେଡ୍): ଲିଭିଯାଇଛି / ଫିକା ପଡିଯାଇଛି (Gradually grow faint and disappear)

  • Valour (ଭାଲୋର୍): ବୀରତ୍ୱ (Great courage in the face of danger)

  • Tricolour (ଟ୍ରାଇକଲର୍): ତ୍ରିରଙ୍ଗା ପତାକା (The Indian National Flag)

  • Poignant (ପଏଗନାଣ୍ଟ): ହୃଦୟସ୍ପର୍ଶୀ / ଭାବପ୍ରବଣ (Evoking a keen sense of sadness or regret)

  • Enduring (ଏଣ୍ଡ୍ୟୁରିଂ): ଅତୁଟ / ଚିରସ୍ଥାୟୀ (Lasting over a period of time)

  • Selflessness (ସେଲ୍ଫଲେସନେସ୍): ନିଃସ୍ୱାର୍ଥପରତା (Concern more with the needs of others than with one's own)

  • Honour Wall (ଅନର୍ ୱାଲ୍): ସମ୍ମାନ ପ୍ରାଚୀର (A wall dedicated to honoring heroes)

1. Summary (English)

The chapter "A Tale of Valour: Major Somnath Sharma and the Battle of Badgam" recounts the extraordinary heroism and supreme sacrifice of Major Somnath Sharma, the first recipient of India's highest military decoration, the Param Vir Chakra. The story begins in August 1947, when Pakistan launched "Operation Gulmarg" to capture the Kashmir Valley, violating an existing peace agreement with Maharaja Hari Singh. The Pakistani army trained and armed about 1000 tribal invaders to enter Kashmir, overthrow the government, and seize strategic locations like the Srinagar airport. In response to this massive invasion, the Government of India airlifted troops to defend Srinagar on October 27, 1947.

On October 31, Major Somnath Sharma arrived in Srinagar with the D Company of the 4th Battalion of the Kumaon Regiment. Even though his left hand was fractured and in a plaster cast, he insisted on leading his men into combat, demonstrating his profound commitment to his soldiers and his country. On November 3, while D Company was patrolling the village of Badgam, they were suddenly ambushed by a massive force of about 500 Pakistani-backed invaders. Major Sharma quickly realized that this was a major attack aimed at capturing the Srinagar airfield. He bravely requested air strikes and laid out ground panels under heavy fire to guide the Indian aircraft toward the enemy targets.

ଏହି ବିଷୟରେ (MCQ Test) https:///mcq ରେ ନିଜର ଦକ୍ଷତା ପରୀକ୍ଷା କରନ୍ତୁ

Facing a devastating seven-to-one disadvantage, Major Sharma refused the order to pull back. He fearlessly moved from trench to trench, encouraging his men and filling magazines with his one good hand. He sent a historic final message to the headquarters: "I shall not withdraw an inch but will fight to the last man and the last round". Moments later, he was killed instantly when a mortar shell hit his position. Inspired by his unparalleled bravery, his vastly outnumbered men held their ground for six more hours. This crucial delay allowed Indian reinforcements to arrive and save Srinagar. For his outstanding leadership, gallantry, and ultimate sacrifice at the young age of 24, Major Somnath Sharma was posthumously awarded the Param Vir Chakra.

2. Summary (Odia) / ସାରାଂଶ (ଓଡ଼ିଆରେ)

"ଏ ଟେଲ୍ ଅଫ୍ ଭାଲୋର୍" ପାଠଟି ଭାରତର ପ୍ରଥମ ପରମବୀର ଚକ୍ର ବିଜେତା ମେଜର ସୋମନାଥ ଶର୍ମାଙ୍କ ଅସାଧାରଣ ବୀରତ୍ୱ ଓ ସର୍ବୋଚ୍ଚ ବଳିଦାନର କାହାଣୀ ବର୍ଣ୍ଣନା କରେ। ୧୯୪୭ ମସିହାରେ ଯେତେବେଳେ ପାକିସ୍ତାନ ମହାରାଜା ହରି ସିଂହଙ୍କ ସହ ଚୁକ୍ତି ଉଲ୍ଲଂଘନ କରି ପ୍ରାୟ ୧୦୦୦ ଅନୁପ୍ରବେଶକାରୀଙ୍କୁ 'ଅପରେସନ୍ ଗୁଲମାର୍ଗ' ଜରିଆରେ କାଶ୍ମୀର ଦଖଲ କରିବାକୁ ପଠାଇଲା, ସେତେବେଳେ ଭାରତ ସରକାର ନିଜର ସୈନ୍ୟବାହିନୀକୁ ବିମାନ ଯୋଗେ ଶ୍ରୀନଗର ପଠାଇଥିଲେ। ୩୧ ଅକ୍ଟୋବରରେ କୁମାଉଁ ରେଜିମେଣ୍ଟର ୪ର୍ଥ ବାଟାଲିୟନର 'ଡି କମ୍ପାନୀ' ମେଜର ସୋମନାଥ ଶର୍ମାଙ୍କ ନେତୃତ୍ୱରେ ଶ୍ରୀନଗରରେ ପହଞ୍ଚିଥିଲା। ବାମ ହାତ ଭାଙ୍ଗିଯାଇ ପ୍ଲାଷ୍ଟର ହୋଇଥିବା ସତ୍ତ୍ୱେ ସେ ନିଜ ସୈନିକମାନଙ୍କ ସହ ଯୁଦ୍ଧକ୍ଷେତ୍ରକୁ ଯିବାକୁ ଜିଦ୍ ଧରିଥିଲେ, ଯାହାକି ତାଙ୍କର ନିଜ ସୈନିକ ଏବଂ ଦେଶ ପ୍ରତି ଥିବା ଗଭୀର ନିଷ୍ଠାକୁ ଦର୍ଶାଉଥିଲା।

୩ ନଭେମ୍ବର ଦିନ ବଡଗାମ୍ ଗାଁରେ ପହରା ଦେଉଥିବା ବେଳେ ପ୍ରାୟ ୫୦୦ ଶତ୍ରୁ ସେମାନଙ୍କ ଉପରେ ହଠାତ୍ ଆକ୍ରମଣ କଲେ। ଶ୍ରୀନଗର ବିମାନବନ୍ଦରକୁ ରକ୍ଷା କରିବା ପାଇଁ ସେ ବିମାନ ଆକ୍ରମଣର ସାହାଯ୍ୟ ଲୋଡ଼ିଥିଲେ ଏବଂ ଶତ୍ରୁର ପ୍ରବଳ ଗୁଳିମାଡ଼ ଭିତରେ ବିମାନକୁ ବାଟ ଦେଖାଇବା ପାଇଁ ସାହସର ସହ ତଳେ କପଡ଼ା ପଟି ବିଛାଇଥିଲେ। ଶତ୍ରୁ ସଂଖ୍ୟା ତାଙ୍କ ସୈନ୍ୟଙ୍କଠାରୁ ସାତ ଗୁଣ ଅଧିକ ଥିଲେ ମଧ୍ୟ ସେ ପଛକୁ ହଟିବାକୁ ମନା କରିଦେଇଥିଲେ। ସେ ଘୋଷଣା କରିଥିଲେ ଯେ ସେମାନେ ଗୋଟିଏ ଇଞ୍ଚ ବି ପଛକୁ ହଟିବେ ନାହିଁ ଏବଂ ଶେଷ ଲୋକ ଓ ଶେଷ ଗୁଳି ଥିବା ପର୍ଯ୍ୟନ୍ତ ଲଢ଼ିବେ। ଯୁଦ୍ଧକ୍ଷେତ୍ରରେ ନିଜ ଭଙ୍ଗା ହାତରେ ସୈନିକମାନଙ୍କ ବନ୍ଧୁକରେ ଗୁଳି ଭର୍ତ୍ତି କରୁଥିବା ବେଳେ ଏକ ମୋର୍ଟାର୍ ଗୋଳା ମାଡ଼ରେ ତାଙ୍କର ପ୍ରାଣହାନି ଘଟିଥିଲା।

ତାଙ୍କ ମୃତ୍ୟୁ ପରେ ବି ତାଙ୍କ ବୀରତ୍ୱରୁ ପ୍ରେରଣା ପାଇ ତାଙ୍କ ସୈନିକମାନେ ଆହୁରି ୬ ଘଣ୍ଟା ଧରି ଶତ୍ରୁଙ୍କୁ ଅଟକାଇ ରଖିଥିଲେ। ଏହି ସମୟ ମଧ୍ୟରେ ଅତିରିକ୍ତ ଭାରତୀୟ ସୈନ୍ୟ ପହଞ୍ଚି ଶ୍ରୀନଗରକୁ ରକ୍ଷା କରିନେଲେ ଏବଂ ଶତ୍ରୁମାନଙ୍କୁ ପରାସ୍ତ କଲେ। ମାତ୍ର ୨୪ ବର୍ଷ ବୟସରେ ଦେଶ ପାଇଁ ନିଜ ଜୀବନ ଉତ୍ସର୍ଗ କରିଥିବା ମେଜର ସୋମନାଥ ଶର୍ମାଙ୍କ ଏହି ଅତୁଳନୀୟ ସାହସ, ନେତୃତ୍ୱ ଏବଂ ଦେଶପ୍ରେମ ପାଇଁ ତାଙ୍କୁ ମରଣୋତ୍ତର ଭାବେ ଦେଶର ସର୍ବୋଚ୍ଚ ସାମରିକ ସମ୍ମାନ 'ପରମବୀର ଚକ୍ର' ପ୍ରଦାନ କରାଯାଇଥିଲା।

Wit and Wisdom: Wisdom Paves the Way – Book Q A Class 8 Jasmine English

Study MaterialBook Q&AAdditional Questions

Wisdom Paves the Way – Let us do these activities before we read. (Page 27)

Question I.

Why do we seek advice of the elderly in our family? Share your thoughts with your classmates and teacher.

  • : ଆମେ ଆମ ପରିବାରର ବୟସ୍କମାନଙ୍କର ଉପଦେଶ କାହିଁକି ଲୋଡୁ? ଆପଣଙ୍କର ଚିନ୍ତାଧାରା ସହପାଠୀ ଏବଂ ଶିକ୍ଷକଙ୍କ ସହ ବାଣ୍ଟନ୍ତୁ।

  • Answer: We seek the advice of the elderly in our family because they have life experience and wisdom that helps them make better decisions.

Question II.

Knowledge is about learning facts and information, while wisdom is about using that knowledge with good judgement.
Work in pairs. Read the following situations and classify each one as either ‘knowledge’ or ‘wisdom’. One example has been done for you.

  •  ଜ୍ଞାନ (Knowledge) ହେଉଛି ତଥ୍ୟ ଏବଂ ସୂଚନା ଶିଖିବା ବିଷୟରେ, ଯେତେବେଳେ କି ବୁଦ୍ଧିମତା (Wisdom) ହେଉଛି ଉତ୍ତମ ବିଚାର ସହିତ ସେହି ଜ୍ଞାନର ବ୍ୟବହାର କରିବା ବିଷୟରେ। ଯୋଡ଼ି ହୋଇ କାମ କରନ୍ତୁ। ନିମ୍ନଲିଖିତ ପରିସ୍ଥିତିଗୁଡିକ ପଢ଼ନ୍ତୁ ଏବଂ ପ୍ରତ୍ୟେକକୁ 'ଜ୍ଞାନ' କିମ୍ବା 'ବୁଦ୍ଧିମତା' ଭାବରେ ବର୍ଗୀକୃତ କରନ୍ତୁ।

.1.Riya’s understanding of plant care helped her grow a healthy vegetable garden in her backyard. Odia Translation: ଉଦ୍ଭିଦ ଯତ୍ନ ବିଷୟରେ ରିୟାଙ୍କର ବୁଝାମଣା ତାଙ୍କୁ ନିଜ ବାଡ଼ିରେ ଏକ ସୁସ୍ଥ ପନିପରିବା ବଗିଚା କରିବାରେ ସାହାଯ୍ୟ କଲା।

.2. Mr. Kumar values time with family over chasing wealth. Odia Translation: ଶ୍ରୀଯୁକ୍ତ କୁମାର ଧନ ପଛରେ ଗୋଡ଼ାଇବା ଅପେକ୍ଷା ପରିବାର ସହିତ ସମୟ ବିତାଇବାକୁ ଅଧିକ ଗୁରୁତ୍ୱ ଦିଅନ୍ତି।

.3. Karuna’s expertise in computer programming helped her develop a successful app. Odia Translation: କମ୍ପ୍ୟୁଟର ପ୍ରୋଗ୍ରାମିଂରେ କରୁଣାଙ୍କର ଦକ୍ଷତା ତାଙ୍କୁ ଏକ ସଫଳ ଆପ୍ (App) ବିକଶିତ କରିବାରେ ସାହାଯ୍ୟ କଲା।

.4. Rohan understood the value of time management and created a timetable to complete his assignments systematically. Odia Translation: ରୋହନ ସମୟ ପରିଚାଳନାର ମୂଲ୍ୟ ବୁଝିଲେ ଏବଂ ନିଜର ଆସାଇନମେଣ୍ଟଗୁଡ଼ିକୁ ଶୃଙ୍ଖଳିତ ଭାବରେ ଶେଷ କରିବା ପାଇଁ ଏକ ସମୟ ସାରଣୀ (timetable) ପ୍ରସ୍ତୁତ କଲେ।

.5. Ramesh chose to forgive his friend for a misunderstanding. Odia Translation: ରମେଶ ଏକ ଭୁଲ ବୁଝାମଣା ପାଇଁ ନିଜ ସାଙ୍ଗକୁ କ୍ଷମା କରିବାକୁ ନିଷ୍ପତ୍ତି ନେଲେ।

.6. Ms. Vaijayanthi delivered a lecture on Indian Classical music at the cultural festival. Odia Translation: ସୁଶ୍ରୀ ବୈଜୟନ୍ତୀ ସାଂସ୍କୃତିକ ଉତ୍ସବରେ ଭାରତୀୟ ଶାସ୍ତ୍ରୀୟ ସଙ୍ଗୀତ ଉପରେ ଏକ ବକ୍ତୃତା ପ୍ରଦାନ କଲେ।

Answer:

Situations Knowledge/Wisdom
1. Riya’s understanding of plant care helped her grow a healthy vegetable garden in her backyard. Knowledge
2. Mr. Kumar values time with family over chasing wealth. Wisdom
3. Karuna’s expertise in computer programming helped her develop a successful app. Knowledge
4. Rohan understood the value of time management and created a timetable to complete his assignments systematically. Wisdom
5. Ramesh chose to forgive his friend for a misunderstanding. Wisdom
6. Ms. Vaijayanthi delivered a lecture on Indian Classical music at the cultural festival. Knowledge

Question III.

Observe the picture carefully. What do you think might have happened? Share your answers with your classmates and teacher.

  • Odia Translation: ଚିତ୍ରଟିକୁ ଯତ୍ନର ସହିତ ଦେଖନ୍ତୁ। ଆପଣଙ୍କ ମତରେ କଣ ଘଟିଥାଇପାରେ? ଆପଣଙ୍କର ଉତ୍ତର ସହପାଠୀ ଏବଂ ଶିକ୍ଷକଙ୍କ ସହ ବାଣ୍ଟନ୍ତୁ।

  • Answer: I think the man has lost something, maybe an animal, and he is not able to find his animal. He approaches a house and finds some women standing outside the house. The man enquires about his animal and wants to find out where his animal is. From the picture, it appears that the three local women are not able to help the man. Because of the commotion caused, a cat and a monkey are watching the scene.

Let us discuss (Page 32)

I. Complete the following sentences with suitable reasons. Share your answers with your classmates and teacher.

  • Odia Translation: ଉପଯୁକ୍ତ କାରଣ ସହିତ ନିମ୍ନଲିଖିତ ବାକ୍ୟଗୁଡ଼ିକୁ ପୂରଣ କରନ୍ତୁ। ଆପଣଙ୍କର ଉତ୍ତର ସହପାଠୀ ଏବଂ ଶିକ୍ଷକଙ୍କ ସହ ବାଣ୍ଟନ୍ତୁ।

Here are the completed sentences along with their Odia translations:

1. Shiv Datt said, “I believe it has been over a week, perhaps even longer,” because it had been a long time since they left the village.

  • Odia Translation: ଶିବ ଦତ୍ତ କହିଲେ, "ମୋର ବିଶ୍ୱାସ ଯେ ଏହା ଏକ ସପ୍ତାହରୁ ଅଧିକ ସମୟ ହୋଇଗଲାଣି, ବୋଧହୁଏ ଆହୁରି ଅଧିକ," କାରଣ ସେମାନେ ଗାଁ ଛାଡିବାର ବହୁତ ଦିନ ବିତିଯାଇଥିଲା।

2. Dev Datt said, “Indeed, we ought to be united in this hope. That is why we press on towards Ujjain,” because they all shared the common hope of finding suitable employment at the King's court.

  • Odia Translation: ଦେବ ଦତ୍ତ କହିଲେ, "ପ୍ରକୃତରେ, ଆମେ ଏହି ଆଶାରେ ଏକାଠି ହେବା ଉଚିତ୍। ସେଥିପାଇଁ ଆମେ ଉଜ୍ଜୈନ ଆଡକୁ ଆଗେଇ ଚାଲିଛୁ," କାରଣ ରାଜାଙ୍କ ଦରବାରରେ ଉପଯୁକ୍ତ ନିଯୁକ୍ତି ପାଇବାର ସମାନ ଆଶା ସେମାନଙ୍କର ଥିଲା।

3. Har Datt said, “Look there, friends. A camel has passed this way. The tracks are fresh,” because he had closely observed the clear and recent footprints left by the animal on the dusty road.

  • Odia Translation: ହର ଦତ୍ତ କହିଲେ, "ସାଙ୍ଗମାନେ, ସେଠାରେ ଦେଖ। ଗୋଟିଏ ଓଟ ଏହି ରାସ୍ତା ଦେଇ ଯାଇଛି। ପାଦଚିହ୍ନଗୁଡ଼ିକ ସତେଜ ଅଟେ," କାରଣ ସେ ଧୂଳିମୟ ରାସ୍ତାରେ ପଶୁଟି ଛାଡିଯାଇଥିବା ସ୍ପଷ୍ଟ ଏବଂ ସତେଜ ପାଦଚିହ୍ନକୁ ନିକଟରୁ ପର୍ଯ୍ୟବେକ୍ଷଣ କରିଥିଲେ।

4. Shiv Datt said, “It appears to be a merchant, running as if pursued by trouble,” because he saw the man approaching them in a great hurry, looking panicked and highly distressed.

  • Odia Translation: ଶିବ ଦତ୍ତ କହିଲେ, "ଜଣେ ବ୍ୟବସାୟୀ ପରି ମନେହେଉଛି, ଯିଏକି ଅସୁବିଧାରେ ପଡ଼ିଥିବା ପରି ଦୌଡୁଛନ୍ତି," କାରଣ ସେ ଦେଖିଲେ ଯେ ଲୋକଟି ବହୁତ ତରବର ହୋଇ, ଭୟଭୀତ ଏବଂ ଅତ୍ୟନ୍ତ ବିବ୍ରତ ଅବସ୍ଥାରେ ସେମାନଙ୍କ ଆଡକୁ ଆସୁଛି।

5. The merchant said, “How could you possibly know that? The poor creature has been unwell for two weeks now. Surely you are a healer!” because Dev Datt had accurately deduced that the camel was suffering from a stomach ache or illness just by observing how it had moved and grazed.

  • Odia Translation: ବ୍ୟବସାୟୀ ଜଣକ କହିଲେ, "ଆପଣ ଏହା କିପରି ଜାଣିପାରିବେ? ବିଚରା ପ୍ରାଣୀଟି ଦୁଇ ସପ୍ତାହ ହେବ ଅସୁସ୍ଥ ଅଛି। ନିଶ୍ଚିତ ଭାବରେ ଆପଣ ଜଣେ ବୈଦ୍ୟ (ଚିକିତ୍ସକ)!" କାରଣ ଦେବ ଦତ୍ତ ଓଟର ଗତିବିଧି ଏବଂ ଚରିବା ଶୈଳୀକୁ ଦେଖି ସଠିକ୍ ଭାବରେ ଅନୁମାନ କରିଥିଲେ ଯେ ଓଟଟି ପେଟ ଯନ୍ତ୍ରଣା କିମ୍ବା ଅସୁସ୍ଥତାରେ ପୀଡିତ ଅଛି।

6. Dev Datt said, “You are free to do as you wish, sir. If the King desires our presence, we shall be waiting under that banyan tree yonder,” because the four men knew they were innocent, confident in their truthful observations, and had nothing to fear from facing the King.

  • Odia Translation: ଦେବ ଦତ୍ତ କହିଲେ, "ମହାଶୟ, ଆପଣ ନିଜ ଇଚ୍ଛା ଅନୁଯାୟୀ କରିବାକୁ ସ୍ୱାଧୀନ। ଯଦି ରାଜା ଆମର ଉପସ୍ଥିତି ଚାହାଁନ୍ତି, ତେବେ ଆମେ ସେହି ବରଗଛ ତଳେ ଅପେକ୍ଷା କରିବୁ," କାରଣ ଚାରିଜଣ ଯାକ ଜାଣିଥିଲେ ଯେ ସେମାନେ ନିର୍ଦ୍ଦୋଷ, ସେମାନଙ୍କର ସତ୍ୟ ପର୍ଯ୍ୟବେକ୍ଷଣ ଉପରେ ବିଶ୍ୱାସ ଥିଲା ଏବଂ ରାଜାଙ୍କ ସାମ୍ନା କରିବାକୁ ସେମାନଙ୍କର କୌଣସି ଭୟ ନଥିଲା।

Answers:

1. Shiv Datt said, “I believe it has been over a week, perhaps even longer,” because it had been a long time since they left the village.

2. they all shared the common hope of finding suitable employment at the King's court.

(ରାଜାଙ୍କ ଦରବାରରେ ଉପଯୁକ୍ତ ନିଯୁକ୍ତି ପାଇବାର ସମାନ ଆଶା ସେମାନଙ୍କର ଥିଲା।)

3. he had closely observed the clear and recent footprints left by the animal on the dusty road.

(ସେ ଧୂଳିମୟ ରାସ୍ତାରେ ପଶୁଟି ଛାଡିଯାଇଥିବା ସ୍ପଷ୍ଟ ଏବଂ ସତେଜ ପାଦଚିହ୍ନକୁ ନିକଟରୁ ପର୍ଯ୍ୟବେକ୍ଷଣ କରିଥିଲେ।)

4. he saw the man approaching them in a great hurry, looking panicked and highly distressed.

(ସେ ଦେଖିଲେ ଯେ ଲୋକଟି ବହୁତ ତରବର ହୋଇ, ଭୟଭୀତ ଏବଂ ଅତ୍ୟନ୍ତ ବିବ୍ରତ ଅବସ୍ଥାରେ ସେମାନଙ୍କ ଆଡକୁ ଆସୁଛି।)

5. Dev Datt had accurately deduced that the camel was suffering from a stomach ache or illness just by observing how it had moved and grazed.

(ଦେବ ଦତ୍ତ ଓଟର ଗତିବିଧି ଏବଂ ଚରିବା ଶୈଳୀକୁ ଦେଖି ସଠିକ୍ ଭାବରେ ଅନୁମାନ କରିଥିଲେ ଯେ ଓଟଟି ପେଟ ଯନ୍ତ୍ରଣା କିମ୍ବା ଅସୁସ୍ଥତାରେ ପୀଡିତ ଅଛି।)

6. the four men knew they were innocent, confident in their truthful observations, and had nothing to fear from facing the King.

(ଚାରିଜଣ ଯାକ ଜାଣିଥିଲେ ଯେ ସେମାନେ ନିର୍ଦ୍ଦୋଷ, ସେମାନଙ୍କର ସତ୍ୟ ପର୍ଯ୍ୟବେକ୍ଷଣ ଉପରେ ବିଶ୍ୱାସ ଥିଲା ଏବଂ ରାଜାଙ୍କ ସାମ୍ନା କରିବାକୁ ସେମାନଙ୍କର କୌଣସି ଭୟ ନଥିଲା।)

Let us discuss. (Pages 36-37)

Question I.

Arrange the following events in order of their occurrence from Scene II of the play.

  • Odia Translation: ଡ୍ରାମାର ଦୃଶ୍ୟ II (Scene II) ରୁ ନିମ୍ନଲିଖିତ ଘଟଣାଗୁଡ଼ିକୁ ସେମାନଙ୍କର ଘଟିବା କ୍ରମରେ ସଜାନ୍ତୁ।

Here are the original events (questions) along with their Odia translations:

1. The King makes the four young men his advisers, impressed by their skills.

  • Odia Translation: ସେମାନଙ୍କ ଦକ୍ଷତାରେ ପ୍ରଭାବିତ ହୋଇ ରାଜା ଚାରି ଯୁବକଙ୍କୁ ତାଙ୍କର ପରାମର୍ଶଦାତା (advisers) କଲେ।

2. Ram Datt explains how he infers from the tracks that the camel is lame.

  • Odia Translation: ରାମ ଦତ୍ତ ବୁଝାଇଲେ ଯେ ସେ ପାଦଚିହ୍ନରୁ କିପରି ଅନୁମାନ କଲେ ଯେ ଓଟଟି ଛୋଟା (lame) ଅଟେ।

3. The King asks the merchant to clearly state his accusation.

  • Odia Translation: ରାଜା ବ୍ୟବସାୟୀଙ୍କୁ ତାଙ୍କର ଅଭିଯୋଗ ସ୍ପଷ୍ଟ ଭାବରେ କହିବାକୁ କହିଲେ।

4. The King praises the young men for their observations and dismisses the merchant’s accusation.

  • Odia Translation: ରାଜା ଯୁବକମାନଙ୍କୁ ସେମାନଙ୍କର ପର୍ଯ୍ୟବେକ୍ଷଣ ପାଇଁ ପ୍ରଶଂସା କଲେ ଏବଂ ବ୍ୟବସାୟୀଙ୍କ ଅଭିଯୋଗକୁ ଖାରଜ କଲେ।

5. Shiv Datt reveals that the camel is blind in its right eye as it had only chewed on the leaves on the left side of the road.

  • Odia Translation: ଶିବ ଦତ୍ତ ପ୍ରକାଶ କଲେ ଯେ ଓଟଟି ଡାହାଣ ଆଖିରେ ଅନ୍ଧ ଅଟେ କାରଣ ଏହା କେବଳ ରାସ୍ତାର ବାମ ପାର୍ଶ୍ୱରେ ଥିବା ପତ୍ରଗୁଡ଼ିକୁ ଚୋବାଇଥିଲା।

6. Dev Datt explains his observation about the camel’s pain and careful movement.

  • Odia Translation: ଦେବ ଦତ୍ତ ଓଟର ଯନ୍ତ୍ରଣା ଏବଂ ସାବଧାନ ଗତିବିଧି ବିଷୟରେ ନିଜର ପର୍ଯ୍ୟବେକ୍ଷଣ ବୁଝାଇଲେ।

7. Har Datt clarifies how he knew that the camel has a short tail.

  • Odia Translation: ହର ଦତ୍ତ ସ୍ପଷ୍ଟ କଲେ ଯେ ସେ କିପରି ଜାଣିଲେ ଯେ ଓଟର ଲାଞ୍ଜ ଛୋଟ ଅଟେ।

8. The merchant accuses the four men of knowing a lot about his missing camel.

  • Odia Translation: ବ୍ୟବସାୟୀ ଜଣକ ଚାରିଜଣ ଲୋକଙ୍କ ଉପରେ ତାଙ୍କର ହଜିଯାଇଥିବା ଓଟ ବିଷୟରେ ବହୁତ କିଛି ଜାଣିଥିବାର ଅଭିଯୋଗ କଲେ।

Answer (Correct Order)

1. (8) The merchant accuses the four men of knowing a lot about his missing camel

2. (3) The King asks the merchant to clearly state his accusation.

3. (2) Ram Datt explains how he infers from the tracks that the camel is lame.

4. (5) Shiv Datt reveals that the camel is blind in its right eye as it had only chewed on the leaves on the left side of the road.

5. (7) Har Datt clarifies how he knew that the camel has a short tail.

6. (6) Dev Datt explains his observation about the camel’s pain and careful movement.

7. (4) The King praises the young men for their observations and dismisses the merchant’s accusation.

8. (1) The King makes the four young men his advisers, impressed by their skills.

Let us think and reflect (Pages 37-39)

I. Read the given extracts and answer the questions that follow.

.1. Dev Datt: Ten days are but a short span in the grand scheme of things.
We may still have many more roads to wander before we find employment befitting all four of us.
Ram Datt: If only we could secure an audience with the King of Ujjain.
I am confident he would recognise our talents and appoint us to his service.
Shiv Datt: I share your belief.
Dev Datt: Indeed, we ought to be united in this hope. That is why we press on towards Ujjain.
Har Datt: (Pausing thoughtfully) Gaining an audience with the King is no simple task. We need to devise a strategy.

I. ପ୍ରଦତ୍ତ ଅଂଶଗୁଡ଼ିକୁ ପଢ଼ନ୍ତୁ ଏବଂ ନିମ୍ନଲିଖିତ ପ୍ରଶ୍ନଗୁଡ଼ିକର ଉତ୍ତର ଦିଅନ୍ତୁ।

୧. ଦେବ ଦତ୍ତ: ଦଶ ଦିନ ବହୁତ କମ୍ ସମୟ ଅଟେ।

ଆମ ଚାରିଜଣଙ୍କ ପାଇଁ ଉପଯୁକ୍ତ ନିଯୁକ୍ତି ପାଇବା ପୂର୍ବରୁ ଆମକୁ ବୋଧହୁଏ ଆହୁରି ଅନେକ ରାସ୍ତା ବୁଲିବାକୁ ପଡିପାରେ।

ରାମ ଦତ୍ତ: ଯଦି କେବଳ ଆମେ ଉଜ୍ଜୈନର ରାଜାଙ୍କ ସହିତ ସାକ୍ଷାତ କରିପାରନ୍ତେ।

ମୁଁ ଆତ୍ମବିଶ୍ୱାସୀ ଯେ ସେ ଆମର ପ୍ରତିଭାକୁ ଚିହ୍ନିବେ ଏବଂ ଆମକୁ ତାଙ୍କ ସେବାରେ ନିଯୁକ୍ତ କରିବେ।

ଶିବ ଦତ୍ତ: ମୁଁ ମଧ୍ୟ ଏହା ବିଶ୍ୱାସ କରେ।

ଦେବ ଦତ୍ତ: ପ୍ରକୃତରେ, ଆମେ ଏହି ଆଶାରେ ଏକାଠି ହେବା ଉଚିତ୍। ସେଥିପାଇଁ ଆମେ ଉଜ୍ଜୈନ ଆଡକୁ ଆଗେଇ ଚାଲିଛୁ।

ହର ଦତ୍ତ: (ଚିନ୍ତାମଗ୍ନ ହୋଇ ଅଟକିଯାଇ) ରାଜାଙ୍କ ସହିତ ସାକ୍ଷାତ କରିବା କୌଣସି ସହଜ କାମ ନୁହେଁ। ଆମକୁ ଏକ ରଣନୀତି ପ୍ରସ୍ତୁତ କରିବାକୁ ପଡିବ।

(i) Identify the false statement from the two given below and rectify it.
A. Dev Datt believes that finding employment is an easy task.
B. Ram Datt is confident that the King would recognise their talents.

(i) ନିମ୍ନରେ ଦିଆଯାଇଥିବା ଦୁଇଟିରୁ ଭୁଲ ବିବୃତ୍ତି ଚିହ୍ନଟ କରନ୍ତୁ ଏବଂ ଏହାକୁ ସଂଶୋଧନ କରନ୍ତୁ।

A. ଦେବ ଦତ୍ତ ବିଶ୍ୱାସ କରନ୍ତି ଯେ ନିଯୁକ୍ତି ପାଇବା ଏକ ସହଜ କାମ ଅଟେ।

B. ରାମ ଦତ୍ତ ଆତ୍ମବିଶ୍ୱାସୀ ଅଛନ୍ତି ଯେ ରାଜା ସେମାନଙ୍କର ପ୍ରତିଭାକୁ ଚିହ୍ନିବେ।

Answer:
False Statement – A.
Dev Datt believes that finding employment is a long journey.

(ii) What does Ram Datt’s confidence in gaining the King’s recognition tell us about him?

  • Odia Translation: ରାଜାଙ୍କ ସ୍ୱୀକୃତି ପାଇବା ନେଇ ରାମ ଦତ୍ତଙ୍କ ଆତ୍ମବିଶ୍ୱାସ ଆମକୁ ତାଙ୍କ ବିଷୟରେ କଣ କହୁଛି?

  • Answer: Ram Datt’s confidence in gaining the King’s recognition tells us that he is confident and optimistic, showing self-belief.

(iii) Complete the sentence given below with the correct option from those given.

In the line, ‘That’s why we press on towards Ujjain’, the phrase ‘press on’ refers to ________

A. walking with heavy steps

B. moving forward with determination

C. slowing down frequently for rest

D. stopping briefly to save time

  • Odia Translation: ଦିଆଯାଇଥିବା ବିକଳ୍ପଗୁଡ଼ିକରୁ ସଠିକ୍ ବିକଳ୍ପ ସହିତ ନିମ୍ନଲିଖିତ ବାକ୍ୟଟି ପୂରଣ କରନ୍ତୁ। 'That's why we press on towards Ujjain' ଧାଡିରେ, 'press on' ବାକ୍ୟାଂଶଟି କାହାକୁ ସୂଚାଏ?

  • Answer: B. moving forward with determination

(iv) Har Datt says, “We need to devise a strategy.” What can be inferred about Har Datt’s character from his statement?

A. He is doubtful of their abilities and wants to give up.

B. He is eager to rush into the palace without preparation.

C. He is cautious and thoughtful, preferring to plan ahead.

D. He relies on others to make decisions for the group.

  • Odia Translation: ହର ଦତ୍ତ କୁହନ୍ତି, "ଆମକୁ ଏକ ରଣନୀତି ପ୍ରସ୍ତୁତ କରିବାକୁ ପଡିବ।" ତାଙ୍କ ବିବୃତ୍ତିରୁ ହର ଦତ୍ତଙ୍କ ଚରିତ୍ର ବିଷୟରେ କଣ ଅନୁମାନ କରାଯାଇପାରିବ?

  • Answer: C. He is cautious and thoughtful, preferring to plan ahead.

2. King: (To the four men)


You have demonstrated remarkable intelligence and the ability to see beyond the obvious. Such minds are rare and highly valued in my court. I extend to you the honour of becoming my advisers. Your counsel shall guide my decisions, and your wisdom shall be a beacon for my kingdom. What do you say to my offer?
Ram Datt: (Stepping forward) Your Highness, it is an honour we had not dared to dream of.

(i) Select the option that is true for both Assertion (A) and Reason (R).

(A): The merchant left the court feeling humbled and ashamed.

(R): The King commanded that the four wise men join his court.

  • Odia Translation: ଦାବି (A) ଏବଂ କାରଣ (R) ଉଭୟ ପାଇଁ ସତ୍ୟ ଥିବା ବିକଳ୍ପ ବାଛନ୍ତୁ।

  • Answer: A. Both (A) and (R) are true, and (R) is the correct explanation of (A).

(ii) Complete the following sentence by choosing the correct option.

The line, ‘See beyond the obvious’ means that the men had the ability to ________

A. challenge common beliefs with reasoning

B. interpret information in traditional ways

C. notice details that others might overlook

D. predict what is going to happen in the future

  • Odia Translation: ସଠିକ୍ ବିକଳ୍ପ ବାଛି ନିମ୍ନଲିଖିତ ବାକ୍ୟଟିକୁ ପୂରଣ କରନ୍ତୁ। 'See beyond the obvious' ର ଅର୍ଥ ହେଉଛି ଲୋକମାନଙ୍କର ଏହି କ୍ଷମତା ଥିଲା ଯେ...

  • Answer: C. notice details that others might overlook

(iii) Complete the following sentence with a suitable reason.

Ram Datt described the King’s offer as ‘an honour we had not dared to dream of' because ________

  • Odia Translation: ଏକ ଉପଯୁକ୍ତ କାରଣ ସହିତ ନିମ୍ନଲିଖିତ ବାକ୍ୟଟିକୁ ପୂରଣ କରନ୍ତୁ।

  • Answer: he had never imagined such honour from the King.

(iv) How does the King’s offer to the four men reflect his values as a ruler?

  • Odia Translation: ଚାରିଜଣ ଲୋକଙ୍କୁ ରାଜାଙ୍କ ପ୍ରସ୍ତାବ ଜଣେ ଶାସକ ଭାବରେ ତାଙ୍କର ମୂଲ୍ୟବୋଧକୁ କିପରି ପ୍ରତିଫଳିତ କରେ?

  • Answer: The King’s offer shows that he values observation, intelligence, and justice.

II. Answer the following questions.

Question 1. What does the dusty road leading to Ujjain tell us about the challenges of the young men’s journey?

  • Odia Translation: ଉଜ୍ଜୈନକୁ ଯାଉଥିବା ଧୂଳିମୟ ରାସ୍ତା ଆମକୁ ଯୁବକମାନଙ୍କ ଯାତ୍ରାର ଆହ୍ୱାନ ବିଷୟରେ କଣ କହୁଛି?

  • Answer: The dusty road leading to Ujjain tells us how difficult and long their journey has been.

Question 2. Why did the merchant become suspicious of the four men? What does this tell us about him?

  • Odia Translation: ବ୍ୟବସାୟୀ ଜଣକ ଚାରିଜଣ ଲୋକଙ୍କ ଉପରେ କାହିଁକି ସନ୍ଦେହ କଲେ? ଏହା ଆମକୁ ତାଙ୍କ ବିଷୟରେ କଣ କହୁଛି?

  • Answer: The merchant became suspicious because the men described his camel so accurately without seeing it. It tells us that the merchant was unable to judge the four men rightly.

Question 3. How do the four young men’s observations about the camel serve as a turning point in the play?

  • Odia Translation: ଓଟ ବିଷୟରେ ଚାରି ଯୁବକଙ୍କ ପର୍ଯ୍ୟବେକ୍ଷଣ ଡ୍ରାମାରେ ଏକ ମୋଡ଼ (turning point) ଭାବରେ କିପରି କାର୍ଯ୍ୟ କରେ?

  • Answer: The four young men’s observations about the camel help them prove their intelligence and gain the King’s trust.

Question 4. Why did the King believe the four young men and not the merchant?

  • Odia Translation: ରାଜା କାହିଁକି ବ୍ୟବସାୟୀଙ୍କ ପରିବର୍ତ୍ତେ ଚାରି ଯୁବକଙ୍କୁ ବିଶ୍ୱାସ କଲେ?

  • Answer: The King believed the four men because their logic was clear and truthful.

Question 5. How does the capability of the four young men make them suitable to become advisors?

  • Odia Translation: ଚାରି ଯୁବକଙ୍କ କ୍ଷମତା ସେମାନଙ୍କୁ ପରାମର୍ଶଦାତା ହେବା ପାଇଁ କିପରି ଉପଯୁକ୍ତ କରେ?

  • Answer: The four young men’s reasoning, attention to detail, and honesty make them good advisers.

Question 6. Why does the merchant feel ashamed for accusing the four young men of stealing his camel? Explain in your own words.

  • Odia Translation: ତାଙ୍କ ଓଟ ଚୋରି କରିଥିବା ନେଇ ଚାରି ଯୁବକଙ୍କୁ ଦୋଷ ଦେଇଥିବାରୁ ବ୍ୟବସାୟୀ ଜଣକ କାହିଁକି ଲଜ୍ଜିତ ଅନୁଭବ କରନ୍ତି? ନିଜ ଶବ୍ଦରେ ବ୍ୟାଖ୍ୟା କରନ୍ତୁ।

  • Answer: The merchant feels ashamed because he falsely accused the innocent, wise men.

Let us learn (Pages 39-42)

I. Complete the sentences by filling in the noun form of the textual words given in the brackets.

  • Odia Translation: ବ୍ରାକେଟରେ ଦିଆଯାଇଥିବା ଶବ୍ଦଗୁଡ଼ିକର ବିଶେଷ୍ୟ (noun) ରୂପ ବ୍ୟବହାର କରି ବାକ୍ୟଗୁଡ଼ିକୁ ସମ୍ପୂର୍ଣ୍ଣ କରନ୍ତୁ।

Here are the questions along with their Odia translations (without the answers):

1. Some word games need a lot of thought and ________ (deduce)

Odia Translation: କିଛି ଶବ୍ଦ ଖେଳରେ ବହୁତ ଚିନ୍ତା ଏବଂ ________ (deduce) ର ଆବଶ୍ୟକତା ଥାଏ।

2. We need to make close ________ of animals in order to understand their behaviour, (observe)

Odia Translation: ପଶୁମାନଙ୍କର ବ୍ୟବହାର ବୁଝିବା ପାଇଁ ଆମକୁ ସେମାନଙ୍କର ଟିକିନିଖି ________ (observe) କରିବା ଆବଶ୍ୟକ।

3. A detailed ________ of the documents shows a lot of errors, (scrutinise)

Odia Translation: କାଗଜପତ୍ରଗୁଡ଼ିକର ଏକ ବିସ୍ତୃତ ________ (scrutinise) ବହୁତଗୁଡ଼ିଏ ଭୁଲ ଦର୍ଶାଏ।

4. You should not make any false ________ against anyone, (accuse)

Odia Translation: ତୁମେ କାହା ବିରୋଧରେ କୌଣସି ମିଥ୍ୟା ________ (accuse) କରିବା ଉଚିତ୍ ନୁହେଁ।

5. She was given an award in ________ of her contribution to literature, (recognise)

Odia Translation: ସାହିତ୍ୟ କ୍ଷେତ୍ରରେ ତାଙ୍କର ଅବଦାନର ________ (recognise) ସ୍ୱରୂପ ତାଙ୍କୁ ଏକ ପୁରସ୍କାର ପ୍ରଦାନ କରାଯାଇଥିଲା।

Answers:

1. deduction
2. observation
3. scrutiny
4. accusation
5. recognition

II. Match the expressions in Column 1 with their meanings in Column 2.

  • Odia Translation: ସ୍ତମ୍ଭ 1 ରେ ଥିବା ଅଭିବ୍ୟକ୍ତିଗୁଡ଼ିକୁ ସ୍ତମ୍ଭ 2 ରେ ଥିବା ସେମାନଙ୍କର ଅର୍ଥ ସହିତ ମେଳ କରନ୍ତୁ।

Answers:

  1. with all our hearts → (vi) complete sincerity

  2. to be precise → (iv) exact and accurate

  3. gain an audience with → (v) meeting or hearing someone

  4. catch his breath → (vii) to pause or rest for some time

  5. erupts in applause → (ii) starts cheering together

  6. grand scheme of things → (viii) overall plan of life in a larger context

  7. taken aback → (i) surprised

  8. more than meets the eye → (iii) hidden information that is not obvious

Now, make sentences using any five of the expressions from Column 1.

  • Odia Translation: ବର୍ତ୍ତମାନ, ସ୍ତମ୍ଭ 1 ରୁ ଯେକୌଣସି ପାଞ୍ଚଟି ଅଭିବ୍ୟକ୍ତି ବ୍ୟବହାର କରି ବାକ୍ୟ ଗଠନ କରନ୍ତୁ।

Answers:

  1. with all our hearts: We sang the national anthem with all our hearts.

  2. to be precise: There were many students present in the assembly hall, 70 to be precise.

  3. gain an audience with: The new online course aimed to gain an audience by offering practical, real-world applications.

  4. catch his breath: After the race, he sat down to catch his breath.

  5. erupts in applause: When the winner was announced, the crowd erupts in applause.

III. Match the different meanings of ‘face’ and ‘bear’ with the correct usage in the sentences given in the table below.

  • Odia Translation: ନିମ୍ନ ସାରଣୀରେ ଦିଆଯାଇଥିବା ବାକ୍ୟଗୁଡ଼ିକରେ ସଠିକ୍ ବ୍ୟବହାର ସହିତ 'face' ଏବଂ 'bear' ର ବିଭିନ୍ନ ଅର୍ଥ ମେଳ କରନ୍ତୁ।

Word Meaning Sentence
Face 1. (n.) the front part of a person’s head from the forehead to the chin (iii) She has a round, smiling face.
  2. (v.) to be positioned with the face or front towards (i) Ravi turned to face his father, when he was called.
  3. (v.) to deal with a difficult task or situation (iv) We should be ready to face life’s challenges.
  4. (n.) the front or surface of a thing (ii) The face of the clock was painted red and blue.
Bear 1. (v.) tolerate (iv) I can’t bear the sound of vehicles honking.
  2. (v.) accept (iii) Riva agreed to bear the responsibilities with confidence.
  3. (n.) a large, heavy mammal (ii) I spotted a bear on a wildlife safari.
  4. (v.) produce; give birth to (i) The trees that I planted in my garden took a long time to bear fruit.

Answers:

  • Face: 1. (iii), 2. (i), 3. (iv), 4. (ii)

  • Bear: 1. (iv), 2. (iii), 3. (ii), 4. (i)

IV. Match the highlighted words in the sentences in Column 1 with the functions they express in Column 2.

  • Odia Translation: ସ୍ତମ୍ଭ 1 ରେ ଥିବା ବାକ୍ୟଗୁଡ଼ିକରେ ହାଇଲାଇଟ୍ ହୋଇଥିବା ଶବ୍ଦଗୁଡ଼ିକୁ ସ୍ତମ୍ଭ 2 ରେ ଥିବା କାର୍ଯ୍ୟ ସହିତ ମେଳ କରନ୍ତୁ।

Column 1 Column 2
1. I will gladly pay your fee if you can cure it. (i) expression of disbelief
2. You must have stolen it and sold it off. (ii) imagined (hypothetical) possibility
3. You shall come with me to face the King! (iii) moral obligation or advice
4. How could you possibly know that? (iv) challenge
5. I am confident he would recognise our talents and appoint us to his service. (v) logical conclusion
6. A camel with a long tail would have fended off these pests with ease. (vi) wish or hope
7. They may tell us more than meets the eye. (vii) necessity
8. May your wisdom shine upon Ujjain and guide us to prosperity. (viii) prediction
9. Indeed, we ought to be united in this hope. (ix) willingness
10. We need to devise a strategy. (x) command
11. You speak of my camel as if it stood before you, yet you dare to claim not to have seen it! (xi) possibility

Answers:

  1. I will gladly pay your fee if you can cure it. → (viii) prediction

  2. You must have stolen it and sold it off. → (vii) necessity

  3. You shall come with me to face the King! → (x) command

  4. How could you possibly know that? → (iv) challenge

  5. I am confident he would recognise our talents and appoint us to his service. → (v) logical conclusion

  6. A camel with a long tail would have fended off these pests with ease. → (ii) imagined (hypothetical) possibility

  7. They may tell us more than meets the eye. → (xi) possibility

  8. May your wisdom shine upon Ujjain and guide us to prosperity. → (vi) wish or hope

  9. Indeed, we ought to be united in this hope. → (iii) moral obligation or advice

  10. We need to devise a strategy. → (ix) willingness

  11. You speak of my camel as if it stood before you, yet you dare to claim not to have seen it! → (i) expression of disbelief

V. Complete the following story with modal verbs in the box given below by using the clues from the functions given within brackets.

may,must,should,can,might,could,need to

  • Odia Translation: ବ୍ରାକେଟ୍ ମଧ୍ୟରେ ଦିଆଯାଇଥିବା ସୂଚନା ବ୍ୟବହାର କରି ବାକ୍ସରେ ଦିଆଯାଇଥିବା ମୋଡାଲ୍ କ୍ରିୟା (modal verbs) ସହିତ ନିମ୍ନଲିଖିତ ଗଳ୍ପକୁ ସମ୍ପୂର୍ଣ୍ଣ କରନ୍ତୁ।

Answers:

An old man, known for his wisdom, lived in a small village. One day, a young boy asked him, “How can I become wise like you?” The old man smiled and said, “Wisdom must be earned through patience. You should listen, more than you speak.” The boy thought for a while and replied, “It might take years, but I’ll try.” The old man nodded. “You need to learn from your mistakes too. Wisdom may come slowly, but it always arrives for those who seek it.” The boy knew he could become wise if he followed the old man’s advice.

VI. Rewrite the following sentences using appropriate modal verbs.

  • Odia Translation: ଉପଯୁକ୍ତ ମୋଡାଲ୍ କ୍ରିୟା ବ୍ୟବହାର କରି ନିମ୍ନଲିଖିତ ବାକ୍ୟଗୁଡ଼ିକୁ ପୁନର୍ବାର ଲେଖନ୍ତୁ।

Answers:

  1. You should think before you speak.

  2. You need not worry about things beyond your control.

  3. You cannot gain experience without taking risks.

  4. You must treat others with kindness.

  5. You must not interrupt someone else.

Let us listen (Page 42)

I. You will listen to four people sharing their thoughts on why they enjoy witty characters in stories. As you listen, match statements 1–6 given below to the speakers (i)–(iv).

  • Odia Translation: ଆପଣ ଚାରିଜଣ ବ୍ୟକ୍ତିଙ୍କ ବିଚାର ଶୁଣିବେ ଯେ କାହିଁକି ସେମାନେ ଗଳ୍ପଗୁଡ଼ିକରେ ଚତୁର ଚରିତ୍ରଗୁଡ଼ିକୁ ପସନ୍ଦ କରନ୍ତି। ଶୁଣିବା ସମୟରେ, ବକ୍ତା (i)-(iv) ସହିତ ନିମ୍ନରେ ଦିଆଯାଇଥିବା ବିବୃତ୍ତି 1-6 କୁ ମେଳ କରନ୍ତୁ।

Answers:

  1. Witty characters prove that cleverness is as powerful as strength. → (ii)

  2. Witty characters show us ways to make learning enjoyable. → (iv)

  3. Witty characters in a story keep the reader involved. → (iv)

  4. Witty characters teach me that tricky situations can be managed calmly. → (iii)

  5. Witty characters add excitement to a story by their extraordinary deeds. → (ii)

  6. Witty characters can de-activate tense situations with their cleverness. → (i)

Let us speak (Page 43)

I. The way in which a word is stressed in a sentence can change its meaning. Work in pairs. Take turns to say the following sentences aloud by stressing on the highlighted words.

  • Odia Translation: ଗୋଟିଏ ବାକ୍ୟରେ ଶବ୍ଦ ଉପରେ କିପରି ଜୋର୍ (stress) ଦିଆଯାଏ ତାହା ଏହାର ଅର୍ଥ ପରିବର୍ତ୍ତନ କରିପାରେ। ଯୋଡ଼ି ହୋଇ କାର୍ଯ୍ୟ କରନ୍ତୁ। ହାଇଲାଇଟ୍ ହୋଇଥିବା ଶବ୍ଦଗୁଡ଼ିକ ଉପରେ ଜୋର୍ ଦେଇ ନିମ୍ନଲିଖିତ ବାକ୍ୟଗୁଡ଼ିକୁ ପାଳି କରି ଜୋରରେ ପଢ଼ନ୍ତୁ ଏବଂ ଅର୍ଥ ବାଣ୍ଟନ୍ତୁ।

Answers:

  • I take my dog for a walk in the evening. → Emphasizes that you, not someone else, take the dog.

  • I take my dog for a walk in the evening. → Emphasizes the action of taking.

  • I take my dog for a walk in the evening. → Emphasizes that it’s your dog.

  • I take my dog for a walk in the evening. → Emphasizes the type of activity.

  • I take my dog for a walk in the evening. → Emphasizes the time of the walk.

II. Statements and exclamatory sentences usually end with a falling tone. Listen to the teacher read these sentences aloud and repeat it with the correct intonation.

  • Odia Translation: ସାଧାରଣତଃ ବିବୃତ୍ତି ଏବଂ ବିସ୍ମୟସୂଚକ ବାକ୍ୟଗୁଡ଼ିକ ହ୍ରାସ ପାଉଥିବା ସ୍ୱର (falling tone) ସହିତ ଶେଷ ହୁଏ। ନିମ୍ନଲିଖିତ ବାକ୍ୟଗୁଡ଼ିକୁ ସଠିକ୍ ସ୍ୱର ସହିତ କହିବାର ଅଭ୍ୟାସ କରନ୍ତୁ।

Answers:

  • Good day, kind Sirs. → Falling tone to indicate politeness in greeting.

  • We regret to inform you that we do not know where it is. → Falling tone to convey finality and seriousness.

  • You describe it perfectly! → Falling tone to express strong appreciation or surprise.

  • You shall come with me to face the King! → Falling tone to command with authority.

Let Us Write (Page 44)

I. You have observed that the kutcha road leading to your colony is in a very bad condition due to heavy rains. Write a letter of complaint...

  • Odia Translation: ପ୍ରବଳ ବର୍ଷା ଯୋଗୁଁ ଆପଣଙ୍କ କଲୋନୀକୁ ଯାଉଥିବା କଚ୍ଚା ରାସ୍ତାଟି ବହୁତ ଖରାପ ଅବସ୍ଥାରେ ଅଛି ବୋଲି ଆପଣ ଦେଖିଛନ୍ତି। ଏହି ବିଷୟରେ ତଦନ୍ତ କରି ଆବଶ୍ୟକ ପଦକ୍ଷେପ ନେବାକୁ କମିଶନର (ମ୍ୟୁନିସିପାଲ୍ କର୍ପୋରେସନ୍) କିମ୍ବା ସରପଞ୍ଚ (ଗ୍ରାମ ପଞ୍ଚାୟତ)ଙ୍କୁ ଏକ ଅଭିଯୋଗ ପତ୍ର ଲେଖନ୍ତୁ।

Answer:

M-52, Mohan Garden

Uttam Nagar

New Delhi

17 May 20XX

The Commissioner

Municipal Corporation of Delhi

New Delhi

Subject: Complaint regarding the poor condition of the kutcha road in our colony

Respected Sir/Madam,

I am writing to draw your attention towards the extremely poor condition of the kutcha road leading to our colony. Due to the recent heavy rains, the road has developed large potholes, and stagnant water has collected in many places, making it dangerous for pedestrians and vehicles alike.

The potholes are not only causing damage to vehicles but also increasing the risk of accidents, especially for children and the elderly. The stagnant water is serving as a breeding ground for mosquitoes, increasing the chances of vector-borne diseases like dengue and malaria. As a result, the residents are facing a lot of inconvenience and health concerns.

I kindly request you to take immediate action. Firstly, the road should be repaired and converted into a pucca road if possible. Secondly, proper drainage facilities should be ensured to prevent waterlogging. We hope you will look into this matter seriously and provide a prompt resolution. I look forward to your positive and swift response.

Thank you.

Yours sincerely,

Abhay Gupta

Which word acts as a verb in this sentence: "Ravi turned to face his father"?

Question 142

Which word acts as a noun in this sentence: "The trees took a long time to bear fruit"?

Question 144

Section G: Long Answer / Analytical Questions (80-100 words)

Question 146

Analyze the character of the merchant. How do his emotions and assumptions change from the moment he meets the four men to the end of the scene in the King's court?

Question 147

"Knowledge is about learning facts and information, while wisdom is about using that knowledge with good judgement." How do the four young men embody the concept of "wisdom" rather than just "knowledge" in the story?

Question 148

Discuss the importance of observation in the play "Wisdom Paves the Way". How does it save the four young men from a severe punishment?

Question 149

Imagine you are one of the four young men. Write a diary entry describing your encounter with the angry merchant on the road to Ujjain and how you felt when he accused you of theft.

Question 150

Question 151

Write a detailed character sketch of Dev Datt, focusing on his leadership qualities and his ability to remain calm under pressure.

Question 152

Draft a formal letter to the Municipal Commissioner of your city complaining about the stagnant water and potholes in your locality, explaining the health hazards they pose.

Question 153

If the young men had actually seen the camel but lied about it, how would that change the moral message of the play? Explain your reasoning.

Question 154

Explain how the story demonstrates the proverb: "Things are not always what they seem." Apply this to the merchant's initial judgment of the four men.

Question 155

In the real world, how can the skill of logical deduction (like the one used by the four men) be useful in modern professions such as medicine or law enforcement?

Question 156

Discuss the theme of "Unity" among the four friends. How does their unified stance help them deal with the merchant and impress the King?

Question 157

Compare and contrast the merchant's hasty accusations with the careful, methodical thinking of the four young men. What lesson does this comparison teach the reader?

Question 158

Describe the visual imagery created by the young men's deductions (the dragged foot, the half-eaten leaves, the blood drops). How does this imagery enhance the storytelling?

Question 159

Why is it important to "devise a strategy" when facing a difficult task, as Har Datt suggested? Provide a real-life example where a strategy is necessary.

Question 160

What role does "patience" play in acquiring wisdom, according to the grammar section passage about the old man and the young boy? How does this relate to the four young men in the main story?

Section G: Long Answer / Analytical Questions

Answer 146: The merchant's emotional arc goes from desperate (searching for his camel), to amazed (when Dev Datt diagnoses the camel), to highly suspicious and angry (accusing them of theft), and finally to deeply ashamed and humbled when the men prove their innocence before the King.

Answer 147: The men didn't just passively look at facts (like chewed leaves or a faint footprint). They applied their reasoning to figure out why those facts existed (blindness, lameness), transforming raw knowledge into applied wisdom to solve a mystery.

Answer 148: Observation is the core of their defense. By meticulously noticing the dragging footprint, the half-eaten leaves, and the blood drops, they gathered the undeniable proof needed to clear their names and impress the King.

Answer 149: (Diary Entry format) Dear Diary, today we met a frantic merchant on the Ujjain road. We tried to help by sharing our deductions about his missing camel, but instead of thanking us, he accused us of theft! We stayed calm and agreed to see the King, trusting that our truth and logic will protect us.

Answer 150: The King was an exceptionally fair ruler. Instead of instantly punishing the vagabonds based on the wealthy merchant's accusation, he allowed both sides to speak, demanded logical proof, and rewarded the men based on their undeniable merit.

Answer 151: Dev Datt is an optimistic and natural leader. He encourages unity ("we ought to be united"), remains remarkably calm under threat ("you are free to do as you wish"), and has a brilliant analytical mind, successfully deducing the camel's internal pain just by studying its grazing pattern.

Answer 152: (Formal Letter format) Respected Commissioner, I am writing to highlight the dangerous condition of the kutcha road in our locality. Heavy rains have created deep potholes causing accidents, and stagnant water is breeding mosquitoes, risking dengue. I request immediate repair and drainage solutions.

Answer 153: If they had lied, the story would be about deception rather than wisdom. It would negate the moral that sharp intellect and careful observation are powerful tools, turning the wise scholars into mere con artists.

Answer 154: The merchant judged the men based on a superficial assumption: because they knew the camel's traits, they must be thieves. The reality—that they were incredibly observant scholars—was hidden beneath the surface, proving that hasty judgments are often wrong.

Answer 155: Logical deduction is vital today. Doctors observe physical symptoms to deduce hidden internal illnesses, and detectives observe crime scenes (like footprints or dropped items) to reconstruct events they did not witness.

Answer 156: Their unity prevents panic. Instead of arguing when accused, they stand together confidently. Their combined observations (each noticing a different detail about the camel) perfectly complement each other to solve the entire puzzle.

Answer 157: The merchant's hasty emotion leads to a false accusation and public embarrassment. In contrast, the young men's methodical, calm thinking leads to truth, vindication, and a royal reward, teaching us that logic triumphs over emotional reaction.

Answer 158: The vivid imagery (the dragged foot, the chewed leaves, the blood drops) acts as a mental reconstruction. It allows the reader and the King to "see" the invisible camel alongside the characters, making their logical process highly believable and engaging.

Answer 159: A strategy provides a clear roadmap to success, preventing wasted effort. For example, simply wanting to pass a hard exam isn't enough; devising a strategy—like creating a study timetable and gathering resources—makes the goal actually achievable.

Answer 160: The grammar passage shows that wisdom is not instant; it requires time, patience, and learning from mistakes. Similarly, the four young men didn't become wise overnight; they developed their incredible observation skills over long, difficult journeys before finally reaching Ujjain.
